```json
{
  "text": "Nine men's morris strategy (team X, first draft).\n\nPlacement phase:\n- Take mill-capable junction points first: 5, 11, 14, 20 (4-neighbour nodes), then 2, 10, 15, 23, 8, 12, 13, 17.\n- Always check first: (1) can I close a mill now? (2) must I block an opponent two-in-a-row on a line? (3) can I create a double threat (two open lines needing one piece each)?\n- Avoid placing a piece where it can be boxed in with no free neighbours.\n\nRemoval choice after our mill:\n- Remove an opponent piece that is part of a two-in-a-row threat or a mobile junction piece; pieces inside opponent mills are immune unless all are in mills.\n\nMovement phase:\n- Keep at least one \"shuttle\": a piece that can step between two points, forming a mill repeatedly (open-and-close mill). Look for moves that open a mill and threaten to re-close next turn.\n- Block opponent's open-mill squares by moving a piece into the gap when they are about to close.\n- Count mobility: avoid positions where our side has few legal moves (losing by no legal move is possible).\n- With 3 pieces left, we can fly; keep 3 pieces able to form a line with a flying threat.\n\nDraw awareness: 40 turns without a mill after placement = draw. If behind, aim for repetition or a draw; if ahead, force mills quickly.\n\nProcess: captain proposes, team votes; after each game the retro captain updates this brain with lessons (opponent habits, which openings worked), not move lists."
}
```

-> Your team's brain is saved as version 1 (254 words).

> Nine men's morris strategy (team X, first draft).
> 
> Placement phase:
> - Take mill-capable junction points first: 5, 11, 14, 20 (4-neighbour nodes), then 2, 10, 15, 23, 8, 12, 13, 17.
> - Always check first: (1) can I close a mill now? (2) must I block an opponent two-in-a-row on a line? (3) can I create a double threat (two open lines needing one piece each)?
> - Avoid placing a piece where it can be boxed in with no free neighbours.
> 
> Removal choice after our mill:
> - Remove an opponent piece that is part of a two-in-a-row threat or a mobile junction piece; pieces inside opponent mills are immune unless all are in mills.
> 
> Movement phase:
> - Keep at least one "shuttle": a piece that can step between two points, forming a mill repeatedly (open-and-close mill). Look for moves that open a mill and threaten to re-close next turn.
> - Block opponent's open-mill squares by moving a piece into the gap when they are about to close.
> - Count mobility: avoid positions where our side has few legal moves (losing by no legal move is possible).
> - With 3 pieces left, we can fly; keep 3 pieces able to form a line with a flying threat.
> 
> Draw awareness: 40 turns without a mill after placement = draw. If behind, aim for repetition or a draw; if ahead, force mills quickly.
> 
> Process: captain proposes, team votes; after each game the retro captain updates this brain with lessons (opponent habits, which openings worked), not move lists.
